<meta>This page continues the philosophical and mystical exploration of the "Mirific Word" (the Wonder-working Word), transitioning from the natural order of the world to the supernatural power of the Divine Name and the transition from the Old Covenant to the New.</meta>

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD

As the same Mantuan poet <note>The Roman poet Virgil, born near Mantua</note> says: *The appearances of their minds are changed, and their breasts conceive now some movements, now others, while the wind drives the clouds; hence comes that harmony of birds in the fields, and the joyful cattle, and the crows exulting in their throats.* And to embrace it in a few words: just as the nature of the universe acts naturally upon individual things according to its prescribed order, so the preeminent mistress of nature, the cause of all things—the Divine Will—acts supernaturally upon all and individual things through its unlimited grace. And just as the former acts evidently by means of sensible qualities, so the latter acts sometimes by means of intellectual names in a confederating way, without proportion, without likeness, and without a measurement of the cause to the effects. God taught us this through heavenly revelation by Isaiah, saying: *As the rain and snow descend from heaven and return no more thither, but soak the earth and water it and make it to spring, and give seed to the sower and bread to the eater, so shall my word be, which shall go forth from my mouth: it shall not return to me void, but it shall do whatsoever I please, and shall prosper in the things for which I sent it.*

Since, therefore, from the matters above, as I believe, it has appeared clearly to us that the Word of God (which by another name is called Reason, since both are expressed by the Greeks with one word, <term>Logos</term> <note>original: "λόγος"; meaning both "word" and "reason" or "logic"</note>) is that by which God makes all things, and works all things in heaven and on earth and in all the depths. Concerning this, I pray you, hear even a mathematician, Marcus Manilius, in his astronomical writings to Augustus, who says in verse: *This work constructed of the vast body of the world, and the members of nature established in diverse forms—of air and fire, of earth and the lying sea—a divine force of soul rules; and by a sacred path God breathes together and governs with silent reason, and dispenses many covenants to all parts.*

And since that Reason and that same Word put on human flesh so that we might more greatly attain our benefit through its salutary touch—when we might discern with these eyes and embrace with every sense that very thing which we could name no longer by an ineffable circumscription of letters as before, but by an utterable voice and comprehensible sound—it was fitting now that the individual pacts of God with our ancestors, every confederation based upon the Ineffable and <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH, considered too holy to pronounce</note> Name, and finally every promise should cease and be transferred later into the utterable name of the visible Word itself, through which He dispenses covenants (just as you received a little earlier from the poems). And not only was it fitting to happen thus, but God willed it so, and predicted it so long before, which I shall affirm from the oracles of the prophets if you bear it with an even mind: *Behold the days shall come, says the Lord, and I will strike a new covenant with the house of Israel and the house of Judah, not according to the pact which I made with your fathers in the day that I strengthened their hand to lead them out of the land of Egypt, which they themselves made void.* These things are read thus in Jeremiah. From this we clearly understand that the pacts entered into with God in the memory of the fathers become hollow and void in later years, and are transferred into another pact. Moreover, out of all the laws, edicts, and prohibitions—six hundred and thirteen in number—the chief pact is this one concerning the Ineffable Name which God entered into with the sons and daughters of Israel, this promise, this covenant. *I am,* He says, *the Ineffable Tetragrammaton, your God, a strong and jealous one.* And it follows: *An altar of earth you shall make for me, and you shall offer...*

<summary>The author argues that the Divine Word (Logos) governs the universe both naturally and supernaturally, and that the old covenant based on the secret Tetragrammaton has been superseded by the "utterable name" of the incarnate Word.</summary>
<keywords>Logos, Tetragrammaton, Virgil, Manilius, Isaiah, Jeremiah, New Covenant, 613 Mitzvot, Incarnation, Divine Name</keywords>

--- Page 108 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument from the previous section regarding the transition from the Old Covenant to the New, specifically focusing on the replacement of the Hebrew Tetragrammaton with a new, "miraculous" name.</meta>

# BOOK THREE

i iiii

through it your burnt offerings and your peace offerings, your sheep and your oxen, in every place where the memory of my name shall be, I will come to you and I will bless you. These things are promised in the twentieth chapter of Exodus. <note>Exodus 20:24</note> And yet, hear again in Isaiah how this covenant was broken: Thus says the Lord, "Heaven is my throne, and the earth is my footstool; what is this house that you will build for me, and what is this place of my rest? My hand made all these things, and all these things were made," says the Lord. "But to whom shall I look, except to the poor man and the one contrite in spirit and trembling at my words?" <note>Isaiah 66:1-2</note> He who sacrifices an ox is as if he slew a man; he who slaughters a sheep is as if he struck off a dog's head; he who offers an oblation is as if he offered swine's blood; he who remembers incense is as if he blessed an idol. Indeed, concerning the ineffable name <note>The Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God (YHWH), considered too sacred to pronounce.</note> through Jeremiah: "Behold, I have sworn by my great name," says the Lord, "that my name shall no longer be called out from the mouth of any man of Judah saying, 'The Lord lives,' in all the land of Egypt." <note>Jeremiah 44:26</note> Therefore, since we have shown that the pact and agreement which God had with his sons and daughters of the people of Israel regarding the four-letter Name <term>Tetragrammaton: The four-letter Hebrew name of God</term> has been reduced to nothing by God's own judgment, it will now be necessary to explain that that name has been changed into a certain other name, which possesses either equal or greater power than the ineffable one. Regarding this, let us again observe the oracle of Isaiah, for thus says the Lord to the Eunuchs: "Those who keep my sabbaths, and choose the things that I have willed and hold fast to my covenant, I will give to them in my house and within my walls a place and a name better than sons and daughters; I will give them an everlasting name which shall not perish." <note>Isaiah 56:4-5</note> It is as if he says: "To my sons and daughters I gave a name which has ceased; but now to the Eunuchs who castrate themselves for the sake of the kingdom of heaven, I will give a better and more permanent name which shall never cease." Moreover, because the translator rightly rendered "place" in this prophecy, know that in the Hebrew a great mystery is contained which has been revealed to us Christians. For there, in Hebrew, the word *Yad* <note>original: "Iad"</note> is placed, which signifies "hand." From this we judge, not without reason, that the Son of God—who is the "hand" of the Father—is promised to chaste men, and that his name is to be given to them. Concerning this transfer of the name, the Lord speaks again to the Jews through Isaiah: "And you shall leave your name as a curse to my chosen, and the Lord God shall slay you, and he shall call his servants by another name." <note>Isaiah 65:15</note> In which he who is blessed upon the earth shall be blessed in the God "Amen"—that is, in the God who is not only the "hand" as mentioned above, but also who is the Truth, the Word of God incarnate who said, "I am the way, the truth, and the life." Do you see how the oracle we brought forward at the beginning fits here: "A child is born to us and a son is given to us, and the government was upon his shoulder, and he shall call his name *Pele*," <note>Hebrew: פֶּלֶא</note> that is, "Wonderful" or "Miraculous." This is that name which we seek. This is that miraculous name of the miraculous Word which we expect, who before eternity and above eternity is God and was made man. A thing first of all admirable: God and man, one person, two natures; or what is greater, in one small body, the Lord of nature and nature itself. He was born, but he also existed before he was begotten. From the female sex, which is human; but also from an incorrupt virgin, which is divine. On the one hand without a father, on the other without a mother—both being marks of divinity. Carried in the womb, but in the womb car—

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD

<note>original: "DE VERBO MIRIFICO"</note>

...known by the prophet, and exulting before the Word by which he was born. Wrapped in swaddling clothes in a hovel, yet he also rose from death unwound from the linen shroud and the face-cloth. Reclining in a manger, yet glorified by angels, pointed out by a star, and adored by the Magi. He fled into Egypt, but he put the idols of the Egyptians to flight. He had neither form nor beauty among the Jews, but he was beautiful beyond the sons of men in the presence of David. On the mountain he shone, transfigured like the sun, and revealed with what majesty he would come into judgment. Baptized as a man, yet forgiving sins as God; not needing to be cleansed by the waters, but rather that he might cleanse the waters. Tempted as a man, but a victor as God, and commanding us to have confidence since he has conquered the world. He hungered, yet he fed many thousands, and descended as the living bread from heaven. He thirsted, but he also cried out: "If anyone thirsts, let him come to me and drink," and he promised to everyone believing in him that rivers of living water would flow from his belly. He was weary, but he promised rest to the weary who come to him. He was heavy with sleep, but waking when the storm arose, he commanded the winds and the sea. He paid the tax, but from a fish. Called a "Samaritan" in insult, yet he healed the wounded man left behind by the priests and Levites. Said to have a demon, yet he was recognized as the Son of the Most High God by the Gadarene demoniac, from whom he cast out a legion of demons and drowned them in the sea. He is the one who saw Satan, the leader of demons, falling like lightning from heaven. Even when about to be overwhelmed with stones, he nevertheless hid himself, passing through the midst of them. He prayed, but he also heard prayers. He wept, but he wiped away every tear. He asked where Lazarus was, for he was a man; but he raised Lazarus, for he was God. Sold for a small price—namely thirty pieces of silver—yet he redeemed the world at a great price. He was led like a sheep to the slaughter, but he rules—that is, he feeds—Israel and the whole world. A lamb remaining silent, yet also the Word and the voice of one crying in the desert. He is bruised and wounded, but he also heals every infirmity and every sickness. Hanging on the wood <note>the Cross</note> he is killed, but by the wood he restores life, saves the thief, and brings darkness upon death. He is given vinegar to drink, but he also changed water into wine. He gives up his spirit, but he also has the power to take it up again. And the veil is torn, because the things that are above are revealed. Rocks are broken, the dead rise again. He dies, but he gives life, and by death he undoes death. He is buried, but he rises. He descends to the lower regions, but he leads souls back; he ascends into heaven, and is coming to judge the living and the dead.

Rightly, therefore, has his name been called "Wonder-working," <note>original: "mirificum"</note> since he himself is most full of wondrous marvels. And know that the Lord has made this, his Holy One—who is the Holy of Holies—wonderful, so that he may hear us when we cry out to him. You have heard what the wonder-working word is. But how is his name pronounced? And is it one of the many previously listed? Or do you ask if there will be another besides? Indeed, it behooves us to imitate the Holy Scripture, which teaches us concerning abstruse and secret things. Therefore, if we hold in our memory the earlier conversations of our disputation, the Word is found in the Father, and the hand in the bosom, and God invisible, and the name ineffable <note>unutterable</note>, like oil in a small vessel. But after he was incarnated, the Word went out from the Father, bowed the heavens and descended, and...

->BOOK THREE<-

...and the Lord sent forth his hand from on high, and God was seen on earth, and dwelled among men. <note>A reference to Baruch 3:37 regarding the Incarnation.</note> And a new name was called upon him by an articulate voice, which the mouth of the Lord named, and his name is "poured out oil." <note>original: "oleum effusum est nomen eius," referencing Song of Solomon 1:3.</note> The flask was opened, lenticular in form with a smooth circumference, and the horn of the "son of oil" <note>A Hebrew idiom for "the anointed one."</note> became a vineyard for the beloved, in which the Son himself is the true vine. Because his name is poured out oil, God has therefore anointed him with the oil of gladness above all his participants and all his companions. By which oracle, by which inner shrines, and by which divine voice do we infer that *Messiah* (which is interpreted from "unction" as *Christ*) is that name—or perhaps some other name which signifies Christ the Son of God with a closer sound and more specifically? For "Christ" is not a proper name but a common noun and a designation of power and kingship. Individual kings and all priests, whoever have been anointed, are "christs." For the Greek word *chrio* <note>original: "χρίω"</note> is interpreted as "I anoint," and *Christos* as "anointed." Therefore, we must accept a more proper name for him, in which we can easily obtain all that we desire.

We are commanded to search for this again within the sacred characters, not without accurate diligence, at the urging of him whose name is being discussed: "Search the scriptures," he says, "for it is they which bear witness of me." What do the holy books contain regarding our cause? I pray you, excellent men, listen without weariness; lift up your minds. For now at last we have drawn near, and we stand almost at the very doors of finding the Name. The history of the sacrosanct Gospel is this: *In the sixth month, the angel Gabriel was sent from God to a city of Galilee named Nazareth, to a virgin betrothed to a man whose name was Joseph, of the house of David, and the virgin’s name was Mary. And the angel, having entered to her, said: "Hail, full of grace, the Lord is with you; blessed are you among women."* When she had heard this, she was troubled by his speech and wondered what kind of salutation this might be. And the angel said to her: *"Do not fear, Mary, for you have found favor with God. Behold, you shall conceive in your womb and bear a son, and you shall call his name ihsuh <note>The author uses a five-letter Latinization of the Hebrew name to emphasize the "Pentagrammaton" theory.</note>; he shall be great and shall be called the Son of the Most High."*

Truly, I will not recount the entire sequence of events, but I will begin from the end as it pertains to our business. *And after eight days were accomplished for the child to be circumcised, his name was called ihsuh, which was called by the angel before he was conceived in the womb.* O happy hour! O auspicious moment of this light, most illustrious men and most loving companions of studies, in which we have found so excellent, so powerful, and so victorious a name! By its nod and dominion, not only the most ample circuit of the earth, but indeed this entire machine of the world and that famous super-worldly region—both the heavens and the spirits of the underworld—are governed. Him whom Moses wrote of in the Law and whom the other prophets wrote of, we have found: *ihsuh*, often desired, long sought, diligently investigated; most pleasant with the fruit of delight and with an infallible bond of charity between us, which I believe will endure for all time. The highest and most surpassing name, a religious name, holy and to be honored; the name to which all sacred names are to be referred; which is above every name that is named in heaven and on earth, and even in the age to come. A miraculous and wondrous name; a name pronounceable by the sound of the voice, no longer ineffable; not the *Tetragrammaton* <note>The four-letter name of God (YHWH), considered unpronounceable.</note> but the *Pentagrammaton* <note>The five-letter name (YHShWH), which the author argues makes the divine name pronounceable by adding the letter 'S' for the Savior.</note>. Nor is there any power in heaven or on earth which [can] resist the name *ihsuh*...

-># ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD<-

<meta>catchword: nichæi (referring to the Manichaeans mentioned at the end of the page)</meta>

...dares to trust. Nor is there any other name established as more holy and more venerable, whose letters are God, whose syllables are spirit, and whose word is God and man. Why do you stand there astonished and dazed? Why do you fix your eyes upon me? Why do you hold your mouths so intent? If you are amazed, let me add even this: that which previously for so long a time—indeed, for so many centuries—was ineffable <note>original: "ineffabile"; meaning "unutterable" or "unspeakable"</note>, has now been made utterable by the joining of a single letter, not a sounding vowel but a consonant. And if you still marvel, I shall add further: when the Word descended into flesh, then the letters passed into a voice. See how openly and faithfully I reveal the entire secret to you.

The grouping of letters is called a syllable; the union of syllables is called a word. You lead me back at last to the basic elements of the grammarians, which, though small to the sight, are nevertheless greatest in power. If we believe Plato, the business of letters is of no small weight; he himself proves their riches—indeed, their delights—with the greatest industry, sharp study, and sagacious wit in the book titled *Cratylus* <note>A dialogue by Plato concerning the origin of names and whether language is natural or conventional.</note>. But let me return: truly, just as in this most excellent name of five letters, part are vowels and part is a consonant, so also the entire word consists of two syllables. The first of these arises from pure vowels, and the second from vowels with an added consonant.

I ask you to recall the Pentateuch <note>The first five books of the Hebrew Bible.</note>, and remember that the best and greatest God willed to be signified by no likeness, no effigy, and no image, but by the voice alone. Therefore, Moses preached to the people in this manner: *The Lord spoke to you,* he said, *out of the midst of the fire; you heard the voice of his words, and you saw no form at all.* And then he adds: *That you might know that the Lord himself is God and there is no other besides him, he made you hear his voice from heaven to teach you, and on earth he showed you his greatest fire.* These are the words of Moses.

Continue listening. The voice is spirit. For they define the voice as "struck air"; thus was Nicodemus instructed by John: *The spirit breathes where it wills, and you hear its voice, but you do not know where it comes from or where it goes.* God himself is also spirit; thus was the Samaritan woman taught. Therefore, the most fitting signs for God are the vowel letters, being named, of course, from the "voice" <note>The Latin word for vowel, "vocalis," is derived from "vox" (voice).</note>. Leave this for now, because the divinity is designated by the vowels themselves.

Now let us pass to the consonant, which we consider more "corporeal" because, by itself and without the breath of a vowel, it has no sound of the mouth, but only sounds along with others <note>A play on the etymology of "consonant" (con-sonare), meaning "to sound with."</note>. Indeed, this consonant letter was not captured by chance, nor seized imprudently; it was not admitted through lack of skill or ignorance, nor unexpectedly or beyond hope. Rather, it was chosen from all characters by the providence of the highest God—which looks upon all things and dispenses all things according to the eternal examination of His reason—and among the Latins, it is named **S**.

Furthermore, if you remember, Baruchias taught us in yesterday’s discourse that the word **Esh** <note>original: "Es"; the Hebrew word for fire is אֵשׁ (Esh)</note> is considered divine, which is interpreted as "fire." This is that greatest fire of God which he showed to us on earth along with the paternal voice, saying: *This is my beloved son.* And He said: *I have come to send fire upon the earth, and what do I desire but that it be kindled?* So that you may understand more clearly and lucidly (for I shall deal with you openly and plainly, not through ambiguities and obscure windings of words), lay this up in your deepest senses: the matter, in my judgment, is no small thing. **Esh**, which in Hebrew signifies fire, is this body—not a divine body, nor an ethereal one, nor a deceptive one, nor a mere appearance, as the Ma-

He uses grammatical metaphors and biblical citations to argue that the vowels represent spirit/divinity while the consonant represents the physical body.</summary>

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD

...Manichæans, Eutyches, Dioscorus, and Timothy "the Cat" <note>Timothy Aelurus, a 5th-century patriarch of Alexandria</note> dreamed up in their delusions; rather, it is an earthly fire, which Moses names as fire upon the earth, when we discuss it according to the **Pythagorean Cabala** <note>Reuchlin’s synthesis of Greek number philosophy and Jewish mysticism</note>. Oh, what a delightful thing to hear! Truly, we shall find that it consists of the same numerical measure as the name of our Jesus of august majesty, with equal harmony and uniform proportion. Furthermore, you will perhaps say that this letter—the *S*—which stands in the middle and forms the second syllable, ought not to be named according to the custom of the nations <note>i.e., the Latin/Greek 'S'</note>, but should be pronounced according to the custom of the Holy Tongue (for so they call the idiom of the Hebrew speech), since we are engaging in a contest concerning the Holy Scriptures. Oh, how I would not hesitate to lead you more secretly into the inner shrines of the mysteries, if I were to answer with the speech of Paul of Tarsus, when he says: *Let it be known to you, therefore, that this salvation of God has been sent to the Gentiles, and they will listen.* <note>Acts 28:28</note> But I shall remain within the Holy Idiom, and thus I will explain the most sacred Hebrew name more easily and clearly, according to the manner and property of that same language. For Baruchias <note>The Jewish interlocutor in this dialogue</note> maintained in the debate—as he himself boasts—that such speech is more fitting for sacred matters.

Come now, if you wish to examine that very consonant letter with precision, you must first take care not to forget the vowels <note>Reuchlin refers to the letters IHVH as vowels here, following a common Renaissance grammatical tradition</note> which enter into the composition of this name. There are only four, **IHVH**, of which the ineffable **Tetragrammaton** <note>The four-letter name of God, Yahweh</note> consists, and which most properly demonstrate the divine nature, as was shown quite extensively and at times most memorably yesterday by this companion of ours. Since these cannot be spoken by human voice or faculty, it was indeed fitting that the God who was to be incarnated should assume, besides those, another character <note>letter</note> with the flesh. By this, the sound that was once unpronounceable and the mark of the then-invisible God might now be able to hiss between human teeth and thus be called "utterable," so that what was previously "un-invocable" may now be perceived as "invocable."

The Hebrews call this consonant **Shin** <note>original: "Sin"</note>, which the priest Jerome interprets as "teeth," writing thus to Paula of Rome: *The seventh connection, which is the last, so that there may be a mystical understanding in that very number seven: Koph, Resh, Shin, Tav—that is, the calling of the head, the signs of the teeth.* <note>Quoting St. Jerome’s letter on the Hebrew alphabet</note> Through the teeth, the articulate voice is brought forth, and in these signs, one arrives at the head of all things, which is Christ, through whom one also comes to the Kingdom of Heaven. I ask you, what is more sacred than this mystery? What is more pleasant than the delight of this sacrament? What foods or honeys are sweeter than to know the wisdom of God, to enter into His hidden places, to look upon the mind of the Creator, and to learn more fully through spiritual wisdom the words of the Lord your God, which are mocked by the wise of this world? These are not my words, but the words of Jerome, a man of such great authority that I may not seem to some to wish to devise a fiction. Therefore, since the teeth are the most powerful instruments of speaking—from which the letter **Shin** is said to be the most celebrated—is it any wonder if all the grammarians, more noble than the rest (to say nothing of the theologians themselves), agree with me that this element and no other was most conveniently summoned for the pronunciation of the Ineffable Name? I know not by what certain agreement the correct institution of that venerable, or rather adorable, name has been [explained] probably by no one, not even one or two of the learned men and masters of divine philosophy—

<summary>This page argues that the unpronounceable four-letter name of God (IHVH) became the pronounceable name of Jesus (IHSVH) by inserting the letter 'Shin', symbolizing the Incarnation. Reuchlin uses the authority of St. Jerome to link the Hebrew letter 'Shin' (meaning teeth) to the physical act of speech and the manifestation of the Divine in the world.</summary>

<meta>The previous page likely introduced the speaker's intent to discuss the "Wonder-Working Word" (Verbum Mirificum) and the mystical properties of divine names. This page transitions into a mathematical and historical analysis of the name of Jesus and the ages of the world.</meta>

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD

<meta>catchword: law</meta>

...hitherto has been observed, by using whose discoveries we might be relieved of no small burden of meditation for this present discourse of ours. Yet there are certain suitable aids and supports for this work which are found hidden in their volumes, upon which one ought to lean diligently, no less for the discovery of a cause than for the confirmation of what has been discovered. An excellent example of this exists, which we recently fished out of a letter by Jerome <note>St. Jerome (c. 347–420 AD), the translator of the Vulgate Bible and a key authority for Renaissance humanists.</note>. I perceive that your minds have conceived no small delight in discussing this name **Iesuh** <note>original: "ihsuh"; a Latinized representation of the Hebrew name for Jesus, often analyzed by Reuchlin for its mystical five-letter structure.</note>; I shall add something of joy so that you may be further delighted and listen to me with a more cheerful countenance.

By mathematical reasoning, it has been discovered that "body" must be sought in the composition of plurality, but "spirit" in the simplicity of units; thus, the closer any thing approaches unity, the more spiritual it is and the more purified from the appendages of matter. We have that most simple divinity of the Word of God, the simplest of all spirits, which is designated by Unity itself. However, its mark—according to the Hebrew teachings—is said to contain those four vocal characters of the **Tetragrammaton** <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God (YHWH), considered too sacred to pronounce.</note> name. It remains for us to seek the "body" of the incarnate Word and its specific character.

Let us therefore look toward plurality and toward the time of the Incarnation, so that from the proportion of the sign to the thing signified, we may emerge more perceptive. First: He who came into the world for the salvation of the world, at what age of the world did he assume a body? You will rightly answer me, in agreement with the Apostle: "In the final or last days." For we are those upon whom the ends of the ages have come.

Regarding the ages of the world, however, different men have handed down various accounts. Ovid and the crowd of poets affirm there are four ages of the world; Hesiod says five, after the likeness of Daniel’s statue <note>A reference to the dream of Nebuchadnezzar in the Book of Daniel, involving a statue made of different metals representing successive kingdoms.</note>; others say six, according to the **Hexaemeron** <note>term: Hexaemeron (The six days of creation described in Genesis).</note> by which God created all things. The rest say seven, as is also my opinion, but those who call the future world the "seventh age" do not divide them according to my view. For then God will create a new heaven and a new earth, and make all things new, as the sacred utterances suggest. Therefore, that will not be an age of *this* world, but a new world—and no longer the **Sabbath** <note>The seventh day of rest.</note> which the Hebrews hold to be the seventh, but the "Sabbath of Sabbaths" and the duration of eternity, not of time.

Now, however, Christ—both the Word of God and God—came into the flesh in the seventh age of this unstable world. I call an "age" a certain state of the world between two notable and common variations, and, as it were, its foreign commotions. 

1. I judge the first to have been when the heavens and the earth were finished, and all the adornment of them. And God saw all that he had made, and they were very good, and there was evening and morning, the sixth day. 
2. Then followed a huge change of the world after the seventh day, when God spoke to Adam: *Because you have listened to the voice of your wife, and have eaten from the tree of which I commanded you that you should not eat, cursed is the earth in your work.* 

Therefore, from the creation of the world until the transgression of Adam, the first age of the world is established according to my meager judgment. From that until the flood of Noah is the second age. The third shall be said to have been from Noah to Abraham and his circumcision, when the people faithful to God were divided from the idolaters by the river Euphrates. Whence Abraham was surnamed **Eber** <note>A play on the root of "Hebrew," often interpreted as "the one who crosses over."</note>, not from the language which he had kept intact for his own from Eber the son of Shelah, but from the crossing of the Euphrates, as if "from across the river." From Abraham to Moses...

<summary>The narrator uses mathematical and mystical logic to argue that while God's essence is a simple unity (the Tetragrammaton), His incarnation occurs in the "plurality" of time. He begins a chronological division of world history into seven ages, identifying the first three as Creation to the Fall, the Fall to the Flood, and the Flood to Abraham.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of world ages and the mystical properties of the Hebrew alphabet, specifically focusing on the transition to the seventh age and the linguistic manifestation of the Messiah.</meta>

# BOOK THREE

We shall count the fourth age from the written law and the sacred, manifold ceremonies. The fifth age extends to the kingdom of the Hebrews, when men began to calculate time by *lustra* <note>A Roman period of five years</note> after the destruction of Troy. The sixth age continues until the founding of the city now called Rome, when individual matters were governed by the uncertain hand of a king, with no law declared or passed, except for those minor decrees which Romulus later brought to the people, which he called the *Curiate* laws. There exist volumes by Sextus Papirius <note>A legendary Roman jurist said to have collected the laws of the kings</note> concerning these; read them more closely if you are able. That age began to count years by Olympiads, just as the preceding age used *lustra*, up until the time of Christ, when the entire world was enrolled for the annual poll-taxes and tributes of the Romans. This we call the *indiction* <note>A 15-year tax cycle used in late Roman and medieval dating</note> down to our own times, containing three Olympiads or three *lustra*, which is now the seventh age. If you judge that I have weighed these matters truly, uniformly, and not sluggishly, I shall rejoice that no small fruit has come to me from my studies. But if, as I fear, it turns out much otherwise, I will at least have shown you some effort on my part, and I will seem at least to have wished to please. Nevertheless, I will play with my own wit, and—just as if the matter were so—I shall posit that Christ assumed a body at the end of days, that is, around the seventh age of this world.

Now, as we have said, the "body" must be sought in the multitude. Therefore, if after the Unity (which signifies not a number but God), you fashion seven "houses" of numbers from all the numeral figures among the Hebrews, and enclose in each house a fellowship of three arithmetic letters in order, it will clearly appear in the seventh house, and in the middle place of its connection, that the very mark *Shin* <note>The Hebrew letter ש</note> designates the mediator of God and man: the God-man who made both one. Moreover, the connection of the seventh house is specifically this: *Resh*, *Shin*, *Tav* <note>The letters ר ,ש ,ת</note>, which is interpreted in Latin as "of the Head." The designation of *Shin* is as if the alphabet were predicting and foretelling by prophecy that the letter *Shin* itself would be the most fitting character for shaping the name *IHSVH* <note>The author's Pentagrammaton: inserting the letter Shin into the divine name YHWH to produce "Jesus"</note>, whom God the Father gave as the head over the entire Church, which is his body, as we read written to the Ephesians.

This, it seems to me, Moses clearly foretold long ago in Deuteronomy, in the thirty-second chapter of that book, when he prophesies thus to the coming nation: "But if you will hear the voice of I.H.V.H. your God"—behold the four vowels. Then, after an abundance of blessing, there follows: "All the peoples of the earth shall see that the name *Tetragrammaton* <note>The four-letter name of God, YHWH</note> has been called upon you." Indeed, this heap of speech—namely, "The Name," "Tetragrammaton," and "Called"—collected together, is understood entirely through the letter *Shin* according to the frequent usage of the Hebrew Kabbalah. Thus, if the last things correspond to the first, this prophecy contains nothing other than: "If you hear the voice of the Tetragrammaton"—that is, when the Tetragrammaton becomes audible or pronounceable—"then the name Tetragrammaton called through *Shin* shall be upon you." It is just as if he were saying: "If the ineffable name Tetragrammaton must become pronounceable, it will necessarily be called through the consonant named *Shin*, so that it becomes *IHSVH*, who will be over you, your head and your lord." Blessed be the God and Father of our Lord *IHSVH* Christ, who drops down upon us from above the knowledge of the true name of his only-begotten Son and our Savior—or, according to the Greeks, our Healer.

<summary>The author outlines the historical ages of the world and uses Kabbalistic letter-theory to argue that the Hebrew letter Shin transforms the ineffable name of God into the pronounceable name of Jesus.</summary>
<keywords>Seven Ages of the World, Roman Law, Sextus Papirius, Shin, Pentagrammaton, Tetragrammaton, Kabbalah, Christology, Deuteronomy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the mystical properties of the Name of Jesus, specifically contrasting its unique spelling with other historical figures who bore similar names.</meta>

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD

For Greek authors derive the name from "healing" and "curing" <note>original: "medendo & sanando"</note>. However, the Hebrew grammarians derive it from "saving," so that they think *IHSUH* <note>The author uses a five-letter Latinized Hebrew spelling to represent the Pentagrammaton: Y-H-Sh-W-H</note> is the same as "Savior." Yet "Savior" is a common noun, while *IHSUH* is a most proper name, so that <term>it</term> <note>original: "qꝫ" (que), a Latin abbreviation for "and" or "it"</note> fits no other except the incarnate Son of God. This is caused by the variety of letters from which the word consists, the likes of which have never been heard since the beginning of time. Within the memory of the fathers, there were many "saviors" called Jesus, but with different letters and different syllables. For no one had ever dared to use these specific letters together for their own name because <term>the fact that</term> the entire divinity was contained within them. 

But whoever was considered endowed with some holiness greatly desired to obtain some letter from those holy characters for his own name, as did Abram. God said to him: "Your name shall no longer be called Abram, but you shall be called Abraham" <note>The addition of the letter 'H' from the Divine Name</note>. And Hosea obtained the letter *I* <note>The letter Yod</note>. For when Moses had decided to send men to explore the land of Canaan, he placed Hosea the son of Nun, of the tribe of Ephraim, in charge of them; so that he might have a more saving strength, Moses changed his name and added the letter *I*, the first letter of the Ineffable Name, and called <term>him</term> Hosea son of Nun, *Ihosue* <note>Joshua</note>, which our people have also translated as *Ihesu*. 

We also read of another from Bethshemesh, whose memory is recounted in the Book of Samuel. And a third with a similar name in the Book of Ezra, the son of Jozadak, who does not keep the *H* but is named *Iesue*. We find also in the prayer of the prophet Habakkuk, in the third to last verse, when he says: "But I will rejoice in the Lord and exult in God my Jesus," which is read again composed of fewer syllables as *Iese*, that is, "salvation"; as if the prophet were saying, "I will exult in God my salvation," which ought by no means to be considered the proper name of God, but rather His epithet or an appositive construction. There is also a fourth, the High Priest standing before the angel, from whom the angel took away his iniquity in the sight of Zechariah; he is likewise written with letters similar to the son of Nun as *Ihosue*, and it is thought that Zechariah then, in that place, saw a shadow of the coming True Light. A fifth is read, Jesus son of Sirach, who lived in the time of King Ptolemy of Egypt, surnamed Euergetes, and is believed to have published the book which exists under the name *Ecclesiasticus*. 

However many there may be who are surnamed Jesus in our common speech, none of them possesses those letters which this incarnate Word of God—our God, through whom the Creator made the ages—possesses. His excellent name is most full of admirable majesty: a name renowned, happy, august; a name most grave, of the greatest dignity, of the most powerful authority in heaven, of the highest magistracy on earth; pleasant to angels, revered by men, and terrible to demons. It is composed of five letters—that is, from vowels and one consonant—by the wondrous craftsmanship of the Holy Spirit. Just as He whose name it is is acknowledged to consist of divine and human essence. For as the rational soul and flesh are one man, so God and man are one Christ. And so the name *IHSUH* is pronounced with the vowels of the *Tetragrammaton* <note>The four-letter name of God: YHWH</note> and the one consonant *Shin*, so that it announces Him who is *Iah* (that is, God) and *Sauah* (that is, veiled or covered). To whom Isaiah speaks thus: "Truly you are a hidden God, the God of Israel, the Savior." It is difficult to believe, most excellent man, if this my weak speech were not strengthened by the sacred monuments of the prophets. Therefore, I ask you to hear Isaiah speaking again:

# BOOK THREE
<note>original: LIBER TERTIVS</note>

->k ii<-
<meta>catchword: more specifically</meta>

For Zion’s sake I will not keep silent, and for Jerusalem’s sake I will not rest, until her vindication shines out like the dawn, and her salvation like a burning torch; and you shall be called by a new name that the mouth of the Lord has bestowed. <note>Isaiah 62:1-2</note> If this title had been common among our ancestors, Christ would certainly not possess a "new name" bestowed by the mouth of the Lord. For the name Joshua—which the Latins translate as Jesus—was first given not by God himself, but by Moses. How then could the mouth of the Lord add a new name to Christ if human invention had devised it previously? 

But lest the words of the prophet escape us, let us see whether a lamp, in its physical form and the shape of the consonant letter *Shin* <note>The Hebrew letter ש</note>, is equal and similar in its physical outlines. Within it, like the element *Yod* <note>The Hebrew letter י, often used to represent the Name of God</note> which designates the Deity to the Hebrews, the wick shines. Its oil joins the lamp and the wick to the fire in such a way that, without all these joined together, the brightness of the lamp refuses to emerge from the darkness. Wherefore Solomon sang: *Your name is oil poured out.* <note>Song of Solomon 1:3</note> Now, among the Hebrews, the word for "oil" begins with the letter *Shin*; therefore, *Shin* stands as a sign for oil—that is, a fitting seal of the poured out and utterable name. This name so permeates the person of Christ that whatever is said of one nature in him is also held to be true of the other. 

Jesus was born a man from a virgin; *ihsuh* <note>A Kabbalistic rendering of the Name of Jesus, inserting the letter Shin into the Tetragrammaton</note> was also born as God from an incorrupt woman. Hence, she is rightly venerated not only as *Christotokos* (that is, the parent of Christ) but also as *Theotokos* (that is, the Mother of God). In this matter, Nestorius <note>A 5th-century patriarch declared a heretic for suggesting Mary was only the mother of Christ's human nature</note> acted imprudently and foolishly, wishing to measure divine operation by logical sophistries. It is a great thing, worthy of wonder, yet it is true: in one subsistence, God was born and God died. Why say more? The Son of God is as much the Son of Man as the Son of Man is the Son of God. 

"Who do they say the Son of Man is?" The apostles answered, "Some say Elijah, others Jeremiah, or one of the prophets." Then the Son of Man questioned them—He who was clothed, He who stood there, He who wept, He who was to be betrayed, He who was to be scourged, He who was to be crowned with thorns, He who was to carry the cross, He who was to die—"But who do you say that I am?" Peter, the summit, the rock, the pillar of faith, and the mouth of the apostles, replied: "You are the Christ, the Son of the living God." 

But why speak so much of the apostles? Profane and unbelieving men might think they were merely flattering their master. Let us listen to Truth itself, which says: "No one has ascended into heaven except the one who descended from heaven, the Son of Man who is in heaven." <note>John 3:13</note> The Son of Man descended from heaven, and the Son of God ascended to the Father. Read Paul’s letter to the Ephesians: "He who descended is the very one who ascended above all the heavens." Listen to Christ speaking of himself: "If you should see the Son of Man ascending to where he was before." But he was "before" in heaven, not in the form of a servant, but in the form of God. 

Now you see clearly what the oil of divinity, poured out bodily, has accomplished: how it anointed the whole Christ. This was not like other "christs" <note>meaning "anointed ones"</note> such as Saul, David, or Cyrus, whom the grace of the Spirit sanctified by working alongside them through a horn of oil. Rather, the Deity, not merely assisting but immanent, saturated the whole of humanity through a substantial and essential unction. Concerning this, the prophet said: "God, your God, has anointed you above your companions." <note>Psalm 45:7</note> Thus it happened that the "poured out oil" provided him with a name more specific and more excellent than his participants and his peers.

<summary>The author uses Hebrew philology and the symbolism of the letter Shin to argue for the unified divine and human nature of Christ, refuting Nestorianism. He asserts that Christ's "New Name" represents a unique, essential union with God that surpasses previous biblical figures.</summary>

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD

...which exists as the name called the <term>Tetragrammaton</term><note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note>, through the letter *Shin* <note>The Hebrew letter 'S' placed in the middle of the name</note> becomes *IHSUH*. For it is with these five letters that the most holy and adorable name *IHSUH* <note>A Latinized Kabbalistic spelling of Jesus: Y-H-Sh-W-H</note> must be written, which our ancestors, abbreviating by the first three characters alone, depicted as *IHS* as is correctly found today in the most ancient manuscripts. This was much like the custom of the noble Romans, who designated their first names with one, two, or three letters. Thus, imitating our fathers, we have similarly grown accustomed to mutilating so noble a word with these three letters *IHS*, rather than including the middle 'S' or the final letter. 

In this matter, many—and indeed distinguished—grammarians of our age, being ignorant of the "barbarous" Hebrew tongue, thought the letter 'H' was the Latin aspiration for the seventh Greek element called <term>Eta</term> <note>The Greek long 'E' (η)</note>, since that letter is often turned into our 'E'. For this reason, they decided in their orthography that this name, worthy of worship, should be written without the aspiration <note>i.e., without the 'H'</note>, not noticing that Priscian of Caesarea <note>A famous 6th-century Latin grammarian</note> established laws for Latin speakers, not for the "barbarians" <note>Here meaning Hebrews</note>. For unless that twin aspiration follows both after the 'I' and after the 'U', how will it imitate its primitive source, the Tetragrammaton, or how will it be truly affirmed to be written with five figures? 

For if any final letter 'S' is detected in either Greek or Latin speech, it must be judged not as part of the substance of this name, but as part of its grammatical declension; such a letter does not remain during declension, but is frequently changed into an 'N' in Greek or an 'M' in Latin. Therefore, there are only five marks by which the most sacred <term>Pentagrammaton</term> <note>The five-letter name</note> of all is formed. Concerning this, we find this Sibylline Three-Line Verse among the prophecies themselves:

->*For then indeed the Son of the great God shall come to men, bearing flesh, being made like to mortals on earth. He carries four vowels, and one voiceless letter within him.*<- <note>original Greek: Δὴ τότε γὰρ μεγάλοιο θεοῦ παῖς ἀνθρώποισιν. Ἥξει σαρκοφόρος θνητοῖς ὁμοιούμενος ἐν γῇ. Τέσσαρα φωνήεντα φέρον δ' ἄ' ἄφωνον ἐν αὐτῷ.</note>

This was translated by us from the Greek into Latin as follows: *The very offspring of God, the great parent, shall come; He will be seen in weak flesh, like a mortal. Therefore He shall bear four vowels, and one consonant sounds with them.* 

Now, your patience, which you have humanely maintained in listening to me out of your goodness of character, has made my discourse longer in its delivery. Therefore, I earnestly ask this of you: that you grant pardon for my immodesty and for this license, not so much in my speaking as in my talking. For I seem to have now sufficiently—and even more than enough—fulfilled my promises to you regarding the Wonder-Working Word, in that name *IHSUH*, the Pentagrammaton most proper to the Ruler of all. He, when departing from us, testified these things of Himself, saying: *All power is given unto me in heaven and in earth* <note>Matthew 28:18</note>, so that we may know, according to the Epistle to the Ephesians, *what is the exceeding greatness of the power of God toward us who believe, according to the working of his mighty power, which he wrought in Christ, when he raised him from the dead, and set him at his own right hand in the heavenly places, far above all principality, and power, and might, and dominion, and every name that is named, not only in this world, but also in that which is to come: and hath put all things under his feet.* <note>Ephesians 1:19-22</note> 

And Paul says to the Philippians: *He hath highly exalted him, and given him a name which is above every name: That at the name of IHSUH every knee should bow, of things in heaven, and things in earth, and things under the earth.* <note>Philippians 2:9-10</note> He was made so much better than the angels, as it stands in the letters sent to the Hebrews, as he hath by inheritance obtained a more excellent name than they. <note>Hebrews 1:4</note> To conclude this burden from all the documents, as much...

# BOOK THREE

Although these words are quite meager, nevertheless, gather from the greatness of my goodwill toward you that this is that Name in which miracles are most especially performed. With this Name as our guide, we ourselves master nature and perform wondrous things without difficulty; no other power of any name whatsoever can resist it. To this Name, the powers of all other adjurations yield, as do the strengths of secrets and the <term>energies</term> <note>original: "energiae," referring to the active spiritual forces or manifestations</note> of the arts. The celestial stars obey it, as do the infernal deities, the natural elements, the silences of the night, the mysteries of Memphis <note>referring to Egyptian magic</note>, the potions of Thessaly, the murmurs of the Chaldeans, the dogmas of Zoroaster, and whatever else exists that is held pleasing by those craftsmen—all of it trembles, obeys, acts as a servant, or certainly shrinks in horror, flees, and hides itself. What need is there for more? This Name offers such great power from itself that, at times, even by its image or its shadow alone, both wonders and great works are performed.

For we read that Joshua—who is also called Jesus son of Nun by way of a figurative tradition, since he bore only a type of Christ and a certain shadowy form in his name—crossed the Jordan, overturned the kingdoms of enemies, divided the land for the victorious people, commanded the sun and moon, and God obeyed his voice. To reach the integrity of the true Name, **IHSUH** <note>The Pentagrammaton, or five-lettered name of Jesus (YHShWH), which the author views as the perfected form of the four-lettered Tetragrammaton</note>, he lacked only this: that his final aspiration was turned into another guttural, and the letter *Shin* was transposed after the *Vav*, so that a creature might not bear the full Name of the Creator. It is thought that he took this name for himself (having previously been called Hoshea) so that by the constant remembrance of such a powerful name, he might perform deeds worthy of memory.

The wise men of the Hebrews have also passed down a similar tradition to posterity concerning Judas, the son of the priest Mattathias: that he took the surname **Maccabee** <note>original: "Machabai"</note> chiefly because that divine verse of Moses was included in the characters of his name: *Who is like unto thee among the strong, O Tetragrammaton?* <note>Exodus 15:11. The name "Maccabee" (MKBY) is traditionally seen as an acronym for "Mi Kamokha Ba'elim YHWH"</note>. By the persistent remembrance of this, he was continually set on fire and gifted from heaven with such great and memorable virtue that he accomplished such illustrious affairs.

Indeed, it was not only the body of Peter, but even his shadow that liberated all those laid out on beds and mats from their infirmities. And the mere handkerchiefs and aprons of Paul, taken from his body and placed upon the sick and faint in that saving Name, drove away not only every ill health but also wicked spirits and their harmful deceptions. Certainly, this came from no other power unless they were either a memorial of the true Name **IHSUH**, or a certain hidden attraction of its virtue, or even the very power of the Name which is the cross of the gallows, to which was fixed the title by the silent decree of the dying man: that is, *Jesus of Nazareth, King of the Jews* <note>original Hebrew: "ihsuh hanozri maelech baihudim"</note>, or likewise in Greek: *Jesus the Nazarene, King of the Jews* <note>original Greek: "ihs ho nazōraios basileus tōn ioudaiōn"</note>.

For just as the **Pentagrammaton** is the spiritual figure of Christ—about which the first Epistle to the Corinthians discourses in these words: *No one can say "Lord Jesus" except in the Holy Spirit*—so the Cross is the bodily seal of the same Christ. Therefore, just as our life is constituted of two parts, the speculative and the active, one of which enjoys the company of the other, and neither is able to flourish usefully without the other, so we obtain this entire divine gift of fulfilling our will through the theory of the Name **Pentagrammaton** and the practice of the Cross. If these are joined together, you will experience nothing more absolute in this vast circuit of the earth. But if they are separated, it is necessary that one stands as the symbol of the other...

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD

...one part is brought forth sensibly and openly in a sign, while the rest is carried mentally in the intention, with an ardent faith joining both together. Does it seem a small thing to you to achieve your desires with such great fruit? This is that **wonder-working word** <note>original: "verbum mirificum"</note> which you have long awaited: the portent-making word, the deifying word—indeed, God the Word and the Word God—and the name of the word **IHSUH**, and the word of the name **IHSUH**, and the same **IHS** <note>A common Christogram for Jesus</note> as word and name, who is the Lord of Lords. This we have learned from Psalm 82 <note>Psalm 83 in modern numbering</note>, where it is written thus in Hebrew: *And let them know that you are your name, O Lord.* For which we sing otherwise in Latin: *And let them know that the name to you is Lord.* Therefore, the same Son of God incarnate is his very name **IHSUH**, which is not different from the name of the Lord, the **Tetragrammaton** <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH</note>, except by the addition of a single letter, **S** <note>The Hebrew letter Shin (ש), placed in the middle of YHWH to create YH-S-WH</note>. This letter pours the divinity of the first syllable into the second, merging and dipping it—that is, the human nature soaked in the poured-out oil, which is why we showed above that the name is "oil." For among the Hebrews, both begin with **S**: *Sem*, that is, "name," and *Semen*, that is, "oil" <note>Reuchlin is playing on the Hebrew words Shem (name) and Shemen (oil)</note>, by which the whole name **IHSUH** becomes pronounceable. It flows down to us drop by drop like the most precious myrrh; and if our mind is properly prepared, it penetrates by such anointing, softens by its soothing, and so fills us with the most pleasing liquid of divinity. Thus, its unction—clearer than the most limpid waters—receives into itself the splendors of all sciences, just as bright water or a polished body smeared with oil can catch any rays of light.

Wherefore, it deserves to be called not only wonder-working, but even prophetic. On this matter, hear the Epistle of John speaking of Jesus: *And the anointing which you have received from him,* he says, *remains in you, and you have no need for anyone to teach you; but as his anointing teaches you about all things, it is true and is no lie.* Considering this, Paul judged himself to know nothing except **IHS** Christ and him crucified. In vain, therefore, do we flee to soothsayers and the secret priests of magicians, to Egyptian disciplines, or any other college of superstition, when we ourselves hold a more certain formula of prophecy: the familiar power of a single name, easy ceremonies, unbelievable powers, and wonderful transformations. We carry in our own mouths the dominion of all nature and the command of every secret virtue in heaven and on earth, and the spirit of omnipotence. Of this, **Jeremiah** speaks in his Lamentation: *The spirit of our mouth is Christ the Lord.*

But let us cast away the deadly secrets of incantations, the vain frenzies, the arts that must be pleaded with, the love-potions intended to force the gods, the Colchian dyes <note>Refers to Medea’s witchcraft from Colchis</note>, and demonic factions. Finally, let us commit to **Pyriphlegethon** <note>The river of fire in the underworld</note>—that is, to the fire and the hearth—the Plutonic volumes, the books of shadows, the Stygian conjurations, and the rites of Acheron contained on parchment or paper. To this fire the authors themselves, from whom this vanity took its origin, are assigned not without their eternal torment. Let us strive toward God, and let us have an unshaken, sincere, and simple trust in the Son of God in whom we live, move, and have our being. He promised us by the infallible promise from his own mouth of truth, saying: *Amen, amen, I say to you, he who believes in me, the works that I do, he will do also, and greater than these will he do, because I go to the Father. And whatever you ask the Father in my name, this I will do, so that the Father may be glorified in the Son. If you ask anything in my...*

He argues that this single name renders all pagan magic, Egyptian sorcery, and demonic conjurations obsolete and vain.</summary>

# BOOK THREE

-> k iiii <-

In my name, I shall do this. These are the divine testimonies, these are the monuments of our sacred rites, these are the priestly memorials, these are the purest signs, these are our ceremonies—content with few things, easy in use, and inexpensive in preparation. This art of ours is higher than heaven, deeper than the abyss <note>original: "tartaro"; referring here to the classical underworld or hell</note>, free from dangers, an enemy to shadows, a despiser of ghosts, loathing idols, needing neither incense nor unmixed wine. It commands all spirits of the dead, ghosts, and phantoms, looking down upon all tombs, all sepulchers, and the appearances of the dead. With a cheerful countenance, it drives away empty terrors, nighttime encounters, and the hauntings of the underworld, overcoming both fate and nature. And whatever we ever desire—if the method handed down by the Master is observed—it incessantly accomplishes and unfailingly fulfills.

It is told that a certain man, lame from his mother's womb and from his cradle, was carried by the hands of his kinsmen in Jerusalem; a poor man, he was brought on a litter so that he might seek a living through his begging before the gates of the Temple, earnestly imploring the mercy of the crowd entering and exiting, because he was unable to stand on his feet. But Peter said to him: *In the name of Jesus Christ of Nazareth, rise and walk.* And leaping up, he stood and walked, so that it might appear how much the name of the Master succors the disciple, and how much imitation profited him. The Master cured all ailments with a word and put all demons to flight; but the disciple also said to the dead Dorcas, *Tabitha, arise,* and she returned to life. These things happened in Joppa; listen now to what occurred at Lydda: Aeneas, who had been lying in bed paralyzed for eight years, was freed by the same Peter saying: *Aeneas, may the Lord Jesus Christ heal you; arise and make your bed.* And immediately he arose.

Paul, in Macedonia, cast out a spirit of divination <note>original: "spiritum Pythonis"; a 'Pythian spirit' associated with the Delphic oracle</note> from a demon-possessed girl with these words: *I command you in the name of Jesus Christ to go out from her.* And it went out in that same hour. Moreover, through the hands of the apostles, many signs and wonders were done among the people, who prayed to God thus: *Lord, grant to your servants to speak your word with all boldness, while you stretch out your hand for healings, and that signs and wonders may be done through the name of your holy son Jesus.* And when they had prayed, the place in which they were gathered was moved.

Hermione, the daughter of Philip the Apostle, and Petronia, a disciple whom Paul taught, having professed the art of medicine, healed everyone by the invocation of Jesus Christ. By that same word, the priest Philotheus, son of Theophila, both turned a stream into wine and moved a great rock by speaking, and soon provided a fullness of bread to the hungry. In the writings of Jerome the Priest—who is famous, I believe, for both his abundance of learning and the truth of his scriptures—you have read about the life of Hilarion of Palestine. While many things therein are most worthy of admiration, there is that story of a certain candidate of the Emperor Constantius, born in Francia in Germany, whom a malignant demon had possessed from his earliest years <note>original: "a teneris unguiculis"; literally 'from tender little nails'</note>. He was led to Gaza with a great retinue and honor to see Hilarion. While the demon explained in both the Syrian and Greek languages (which the man had never learned) the complex reason for the possession, the holy man replied: "I do not care how you entered, but I command that you exit in the name of our Lord Jesus Christ." Thus also, by invoking Jesus, he restored three corpses of boys to life and health. Almost the entire book is full of wonderful things; consider also in your minds how much power of the cross the town of Epidaurus in Dalmatia felt during that greatest earthquake, when the receding seas threatened a flood. For when already with a whirlpool of waves and...

-># ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD<-

with a gentle wave, and though the mountains were being nearly overturned by the mass of stones, Hilarion <note>St. Hilarion (291–371 AD), an anchorite who spent most of his life in the desert.</note> drew three signs of the cross in the sand with his hand extended against the flood; and soon the sea, swelling to an incredible height, gradually sank back into itself before him. I am silent regarding the power with which he repelled pirates rushing upon him, and how he tamed a raging camel with the Syrian tongue <note>original: "sermone Syro" - likely referring to Aramaic or a related dialect</note>, and how, having invoked the name **IHSUH**, he freed Orion from a legion of demons who were crying out within him with the diverse voices of a multitude. Indeed, the demons tremble even to this day when the name of Christ is invoked, as Nazianzen <note>Gregory of Nazianzus, a 4th-century Church Father.</note> says in his defense. Nor has our own malice caused the virtue and power of so great a name to vanish since the time when Christ spoke (as Mark witnesses at the conclusion of his Gospel): *In my name they shall cast out demons.* Although those words are not found in the Greek codices, nor is that entire passage which begins, *But rising early on the first day of the week* <note>original: "Resurgens autē ihsuh mane prima sabbati"</note> until the end—which Jerome also mentions to the Gallic woman Hedibia, and Hilary somewhat supports in the tenth book of *On the Trinity*, and Eusebius Pamphili fixed the end of the Gospel there where it is written: *And they said nothing to anyone, for they were afraid.* 

In Luke, however, we read without controversy that the seventy-two returned with joy, saying: *Lord, even the demons are subject to us in your name.* And he said to them: *Behold, I have given you power to tread upon serpents and scorpions, and over all the power of the enemy, and nothing shall hurt you.* Thus Paul, on the island of Malta <note>original: "Meleta"</note>—which certain faulty Latin books call Mitylene—shook off into the fire, unharmed, a viper clinging to his arm. For sailing from Cnidus toward Syracuse, how could he have reached Lesbos? If you will listen not unwillingly, I will enumerate for you the places of his pilgrimage, so that it may become more manifest that it was not Mitylene but Malta, that island in which and around which Paul showed such great miracles by land and sea. 

From Damascus he went to Jerusalem, thence to Tarsus, after that to Antioch, again to Jerusalem and again to Antioch; there, set apart for the apostleship with Barnabas, he reached as far as Seleucia, thence to Cyprus where he began to be called Paul, afterward Perga and Antioch of Pisidia and Iconium and Lystra, and Derbe of Lycaonia, and Pamphylia, and again Perga and Attalia, and likewise a third time to Antioch of Syria, and a third time to Jerusalem on account of the circumcision, again a fourth time to Antioch, then he passed through Syria and Cilicia, coming a second time to Derbe and Lystra where he circumcised Timothy, after that he entered Phrygia and Galatia and Mysia and Troas and Neapolis and Philippi of Macedonia, Amphipolis, Apollonia, Thessalonica, Berea, Athens, Corinth, Ephesus, Caesarea, and a second time to Antioch of Pisidia, and a second time to Ephesus; traveling thence through Macedonia he returned a second time to the Philippians, and again to the region of Troy where he raised Eutychus from the dead, thence he came to Assos and Mitylene and toward Chios, and to Samos, and Miletus, and Cos, and Rhodes, and Patara, and Tyre, and Ptolemais, and Caesarea, and a fourth time to Jerusalem, whence he was sent to Caesarea and bound, at last to Rome. In this manner from Caesarea he indeed touched at Sidon, thence Myra of Lycia, hence to Cnidus, then finally to the island which is called Malta where he cast away the serpent clinging to his body without harm, from which place he was led to Syracuse, and Rhegium, and Puteoli, and finally on foot...

<summary>The text argues for the miraculous power of the name of Jesus (IHSUH), citing the deeds of St. Hilarion and St. Paul, while providing a detailed geographical itinerary of Paul's travels to correct a common misidentification of the island of Malta.</summary>

BOOK THREE

progressing toward Rome, so that by traveling ever further and wider, the knowledge of so great a name might become more illustrious among various nations; for indeed, all would perceive that in this one specific name, such great power had been divinely granted and entrusted to men for performing wonders. Thus Sylvester <note>Pope Sylvester I (d. 335 AD), who according to legend bound a dragon in the Roman Forum.</note> shut up the dragon in the Capitol, and Philip put the Leviathan to flight in the name of Jesus. John the Evangelist, having overcome poison, expelled from that place a demon who had dwelled in the temple of Diana for two hundred and forty-nine years, using these words: "I forbid you in the name of Jesus Christ the Nazarene to dwell here any longer," and immediately the demon departed from Ephesus.

Because of this, by the order of Domitian, the holy man was deported into exile to the island of Patmos, not without his companion Prochorus. There he performed such great miracles through the name of Jesus that the priests of Apollo, struck at once by impatience and the fear of future abandonment, brought the whole matter with screeching complaints to Cynops, easily the chief of the magicians, who lived in a certain deserted place near Phora, a town on that island. To relieve them of this annoyance, Cynops promised to send evil angels against John to kill him. And when he had sent one of the malignant spirits to Phora, to whom he had enjoined the cruel task of murder, and as it was already gliding into the house of Myron where John was, John himself, foreseeing the cause, said to the demon: "I forbid you in the name of Jesus Christ to move from this spot until you openly profess for what purpose you have come." At the spoken word, the spirit stood still, as if bound by a chain.

And again John said, "Come, tell me why you have entered this house." The spirit replied, "Because the priests of Apollo met with Cynops with lamentation, preaching many evils about you. While they urged him to come into the city to kill you, Cynops said that he had sat in that wilderness for many years, and certainly had never entered the city even for a greater cause; therefore, for the sake of this little man <note>original: "homuncionis" - a diminutive, dismissive term for John.</note>, it was not right for him to go now. Therefore, commanding me as I was about to go, he ordered that I should bring your soul to him, which he prepared to hand over to judgment."

But John replied, "Have you ever been sent by him before to take someone's soul?" And the spirit answered, "Certainly I have been sent, and I have killed as well, but I have carried away no soul." Then John said, "Why do you obey him?" And the spirit, responding to this, said, "All the power of Satan dwells there, and he has entered into a conspiracy with all the principalities, and we ourselves are likewise with him; and Cynops obeys us, and we in turn obey him." And John said, "Listen, malignant spirit: John, the Apostle of the Son of God, Jesus Christ, commands you never hereafter to go out to waylay a man, nor to depart from this place."

Because that spirit did not return to the Magician, he sent another who met with a similar fate. He sent again two of the princes <note>referring to high-ranking demons or "principalities."</note>, one of whom entered the house while the other stood outside to watch what would happen. But the one who entered remained by a similar command, while the one who stood at the door returned quickly to announce the fact to the Magician. At this, Cynops, inflamed with fury and taking his entire cohort of demons, went into the city; and the whole city applauded seeing Cynops, and everyone adored him. But when he learned that John was teaching publicly, he was filled there with the burning smoke of wrath, and said to the people: "O men, blind and wandering from the truth..."

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD
<note>original: "DE VERBO MIRIFICO"</note>

Listen to the way: if John is honest and his words are right, he will care for both you and me through the speech I shall address to him. I myself will also believe his words and deeds. Then, grabbing a certain youth, he asked whether his father was alive. When the youth replied that he was dead, he asked, "By what death?" The boy answered that because he was a sailor, he had perished some time ago, suffocated in the sea by the force of the waves. Then Cynops, turning to John, said: "Now show if the things you preach are true; bring back this youth's father from the sea and restore him alive to this boy and to us all."

John answered him, "Christ did not send me to raise the dead, but to teach those who are wandering astray." Then the Magician <note>Cynops</note>, boasting to the common people, spoke these words: "Now, now you men of Phoros <note>The inhabitants of the main port of Patmos</note> see what a deceiver this man is, who misleads you with magical illusions. Seize and hold him until I restore the father alive to the youth." So they held John. 

Cynops, however, with palms outstretched toward the shores of the sea and stomping his feet, produced a sound like a great crash in the waters, and thus he vanished from their sight. All those standing by cried out with a raised voice, "Great art thou, Cynops, and there is no one besides thee!" Presently the Magician ascended from the waters, having with him (as he himself claimed) the boy's parent, and everyone was struck with astonished wonder. He said to the boy, "Is this your father?" He replied, "Certainly, lord, it is he." They all began to worship Cynops and determined to kill John, but the Magician forbade it, saying, "After you have seen much greater things than these, then finally you may afflict him most terribly."

Calling another man to him, he asked, "Do you have a son?" The man said, "Certainly, lord, I had one, but he was killed by slaughter and treachery." The magician said to him, "Your son shall live again," and lifting his voice, he called by name both the murderer and the slain man. Immediately both stood there together. Cynops said to that man, "Is this your son, and is this the one who killed him?" The man replied, "Indeed, certainly, lord." 

Looking at John, the Magician said, "Why do you marvel?" John replied, "I do not marvel at these things at all." And again the Magician said, "You shall see greater things than these, and then you will be amazed." To which John spoke thus: "Truly, your signs shall quickly be dissolved." Hearing this speech, the crowd squeezed the holy man so tightly that he fell as if dead. Cynops, thinking him already dead, cried out, "Do not bury him under the earth; let the birds of heaven devour him, and let us see then if his Christ will raise him up!" Firmly believing that he was dead, they each departed together with Cynops, praising and glorifying him most grandly.

But when the Magician heard that John had gone to the place in the city called *Lithobolus* <note>The "Stone-thrower" or "Stony Place"</note> to preach and teach there, he approached again with his demons, by whose ministry he practiced Necromancy <term>Necromancy: the practice of communicating with or raising the dead</term>, not without a great crowd of following men. Mixed among them walked those three "resurrected" men, who, although they were demons, were thought to be human beings. 

The Magician said to John, "Behold, I forbade you to be killed so that I might affect you with greater shame and fill your face with ignominy. Therefore, come if you please to the port so that you may behold my power, and you will wonder even more." Therefore John returned with the entire multitude, commanding those three demons who accompanied them under the image of recently risen men...

<summary>The sorcerer Cynops performs several false resurrections to discredit the Apostle John, eventually leaving John for dead before discovering he is still preaching. The narrative reveals that Cynops's "miracles" are actually demonic deceptions.</summary>

-># BOOK THREE<-

so that they would not leave. Then, with a clapping of hands and a terrible sound rising from the sea, Cynops <note>A legendary sorcerer and antagonist of St. John on the island of Patmos</note> lowered himself into the water as before and vanished from the sight of all. The people did not cease crying out in praise, saying, "Great are you, Cynops, and there is none besides you." John, meanwhile, prayed to the Lord that this Magician should no longer be numbered among the living. Then suddenly, a violent roaring of the sea was heard, and as storms rushed upon the place where he had cast himself, he appeared no more. And the demons who stood there in human form—as if they had long ago been raised from the dead—were adjured by the Apostle of God, saying: "In the name of the crucified Jesus Christ <note>original: "ihsuh chrifti"</note>, leave this island never to return." Immediately they vanished from the eyes of all who were waiting for Cynops to see if he might rise again from the depths. And when they had remained in that place for three days and three nights, many of them, weakened by hunger, the noise, and the heat, lay falling upon the ground as if mute. John took pity on them and finally raised three young men who had died, preaching to the people those things which lead to salvation. They, approaching Christ, were baptized, leaving the Magician behind like a second Pharaoh in the depths of the sea.

I could recite many more things to you concerning that holy Evangelist: what kind of and how many virtues he showed through this most sacred and miraculous Name of ours, both on land and on the deep; and how, returning from the island after the death of the Emperor Domitian, he raised his holy hostess Drusiana from the dead by his voice alone, saying: "Jesus Christ <note>original: "ihsuh chriftus"</note> awakens you, Drusiana; rise and go home to prepare a meal for me." So too the Apostle Thomas raised a woman who had been dead a long time. And in the name of Jesus, Bartholomew shattered the idol of the sun and visibly expelled a demon from it. But the whole day would fail me, even one just begun, and you would perhaps be affected by weariness from such a long discourse.

Let it suffice to have proposed these things so that you may understand that just as the Father is in the Son, and the Son is in men, so the name of the Father is glorified through the Son, and the name of the Son is glorified through men. The name of the Son and the Father is one, except for a consonant letter, just as the Son and the Father are one, except for the nature of humanity. Therefore he truly said: *O Father, I have manifested your name to men, because the words which you gave to me I have given to them, that they may be one as we are one. I in them and you in me, that they may be perfected into one; and I have made known your name to them and will make it known, so that the love with which you loved me may be in them, and I in them.*

You are no longer unskilled, since you have heard the perfect doctrine of every stupendous operation and all wonderful things. This is the place of that art; this is the foundation; this is the discipline; this is the **Philosophers' Stone** <note>The "lapis philofophorum," here used metaphorically to describe the Divine Name as the ultimate source of power</note>, far surpassing that about which the wandering Alchemists <note>original: "Archimi ci"</note> contend. This is the voice. These are the characters by which omnipotence is invoked for performing things beyond the common mode of life. Concerning which Zechariah said: *He shall invoke my name, and I will hear him.* By three characters in the time of Nature, by four characters in the time of the Law, and by five characters in the time of Grace, the divine omnipotence has been invoked. For the Lord spoke to Moses, saying: "I am the **Four-Lettered Name** <note>original: "Tetragrammus," referring to the Hebrew YHWH</note> who appeared to Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob as God Almighty <note>original: "deo Sadai"</note>, and my name **Lord** <note>original: "Adonai"</note>..."
