->ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD<-

<note>original: "DE VERBO MIRIFICO"</note>

...and I revealed to them. And it follows: *And I will take you to me for a people, and I will be your God, and you shall know that I am Adonai.* You have heard Moses; now hear the Evangelist and the Apostle of God. This, he says, is the command of God: that we should believe in the name of his son, **IHSVH** <note>The "Pentagrammaton" or five-letter name of Jesus, central to Reuchlin's Christian Kabbalah</note> Christ. Join all these things together, and you will easily recognize that the most powerful virtue and the most effective operation has appeared everywhere through the name of the ancestors, the **Trigrammaton** <note>The three-letter name: Shaddai (SDI)</note>, and of the fathers, the **Tetragrammaton** <note>The four-letter name: Yahweh (ADNI/YHVH)</note>, and of the sons, the **Pentagrammaton** <note>The five-letter name: Jesus (IHSVH)</note>. That is: in Nature, **S D I**; in the Law, **A D N I**; in Love, **IHSVH**. For now we are the sons of God, and for that reason we use five letters, because of him in whom we believe and whom we invoke as the mediator between God and man—the same being both God and man, the arm and power of the most high God. Baruchias revealed enough and more than enough to us yesterday regarding this five-fold number <term>quinarius</term> <note>The number five, representing the five-letter name of Jesus</note>.

If you wish, therefore, to operate all things securely, I advise you—not without reason, as Paul did to the Colossians—that whatever you do in word and in deed, do it all in the name of our Lord **IHSVH**. This is the bringer of health; this is the medicine; or rather, this is our salvation and our cure. Thus Peter speaks to the leaders of the people and the elders of Israel concerning the man restored to health: *Be it known to all of you and to all the people of Israel, that in the name of our Lord IHSVH Christ of Nazareth, whom you crucified, whom God raised from the dead, by him this man stands before you whole.* For there is no other name under heaven given to men by which we must be saved. This is the courtyard, this the portico, this the door, this the first gate to God, who commanded: *Be sanctified, be clean.* This is the bath by which we are cleansed of filth before God. Paul makes this known to the Corinthians: *You are washed, you are sanctified, and you are justified in the name of our Lord IHSVH Christ.* And that disciple says: *I write to you, little children, because your sins are forgiven you for his name's sake.*

This is our adoption; for God gave us the power to become sons of God, we who believe in his name. For so it is placed in the Greek, and not as it is commonly read, "In his name." This is our reward. For whoever gives a drink of water in that name shall not lose his reward. And everyone who has left house, or brothers, or sisters, or father, or mother, or wife, or children, or lands for that name's sake, shall receive a hundredfold and shall possess eternal life. This is the fulfillment of our will. For Christ promised, saying: *If you ask the Father anything in my name, he will give it to you.* This is our consolation. For the Paraclete—that is, the Comforter and Encourager, the Holy Spirit—is sent by the Father to us mortals in the name of **IHSVH**. This is our life, as the Evangelist writes: *That you may know that you have eternal life in the name of his Son.* This is our religion, this our reverence: that in the name of **IHSVH** every knee should bow, of things in heaven, on earth, and under the earth. This is the vengeance and punishment against the impious, because they are condemned immediately because they do not believe in the name of the only-begotten Son. This is perfect joy for the pious: *Until now you have not asked anything in that name; ask, that your joy may be full.* This is a vehement and strong adjuration and exorcism: *I beseech you, brothers, by the name of our Lord IHSVH Christ.* This is our boldness, this our confidence. For Paul in Damascus acted confidently in all things in the name of **IHSVH**, and he returned...

<summary>This page argues that the five-letter name of Jesus (IHSVH) is the supreme "Pentagrammaton," succeeding the three and four-letter names of God to provide salvation, healing, and spiritual power. It cites numerous New Testament passages to prove that all Christian life—from baptism to exorcism—operates through the power of this specific Name.</summary>

# BOOK THREE

<margin>THE END</margin>Entering and leaving Jerusalem with his disciples, acting faithfully in that name. What more is there to say? This is the presence of God who said, *Wherever two or three are gathered in my name, there I am in the midst of them* <note>Matthew 18:20</note>. But believe me, there is no Christ without the cross. His cross is our victory. And Moses, with his hands raised, prefigured the cross and Amalek was defeated; and in Ezekiel, the *Tau* <note>The Hebrew letter T, often associated with the sign of the cross in medieval typology</note> of the cross saved those who were marked. Although the cross among the most ancient peoples—as it is written to be contained in a certain college of the Arabs—is an old character of the Magi, yet because we read of none of its powers being effective without the name of Jesus, we therefore most rightly seek and find both Jesus with the cross and the cross with Jesus, by the testimony of Bishop Martin <note>Likely St. Martin of Tours</note>. This is the sign of the Son of Man which will be seen in heaven. This is the second tree of Paradise, the tree of life, a tree sweet to eat, and beautiful to the eyes and delightful to the sight, not forbidden but offered: *On whatever day you eat from it, your eyes shall be opened and you shall be as gods* <note>A mystical inversion of Genesis 3:5, suggesting the Cross restores what was lost</note>. This is the wondrous wood <term>lignum: wood or tree</term> that the Lord showed to Moses, through which he turned bitter waters into sweetness. For so it is read: *And the people murmured against Moses, saying, "What shall we drink?" But he cried out to the Lord, who showed him a piece of wood.* What need was there in the desert of Shur and the woody solitude to show a piece of wood, when so many trees, branches, trunks, oaks, and shrubs were ready and at hand? But in the Hebrew, a word is placed in this location <note>original: "yarah"</note> which signifies to institute, to demonstrate, to teach, and to set down a law, by which it may be understood that God, by a certain secret doctrine, revealed to his prophet the power of this cross, which is named by a similar word among the Hebrews. Therefore the scripture does not say, "Take for yourself a piece of wood," but God *showed* him the wood, which could be read thus: *The Lord instructed him concerning the cross* or *God demonstrated the cross to him.* This is that "face" which the Royal Prophet <note>King David</note> desires upon the lilies or roses, modulating this half-verse: *Show your face and we shall be saved.* For if neither the Pythagorean teachings of the philosophers nor the Cabalistic teachings of the Hebrews displease you, you will find that the statue, the wood, the cross, and "your face" are the same thing, over which you heard David crying out to the Lord. With the cross, therefore, and not without the cross, all our operation will be easy. Those who act otherwise lose their labor and expense; they work in vain, are close to danger, and suffer the losses of their own vanity. For certain of the traveling Jewish exorcists also attempted to invoke the name of the Lord Jesus over those who had evil spirits, saying: *I adjure you by Jesus whom Paul preaches.* But the wicked spirit answering said to them: *Jesus I know, and Paul I know, but who are you?* And the man in whom the most evil demon resided, leaping upon them and mastering them both, prevailed against them, so that they fled from that house naked and wounded. These things are written in the second book of Luke <note>The Acts of the Apostles, traditionally attributed to Luke</note>. Accordingly, those not observing the manner of the cross, and the law known to the faithful, will perform nothing worthy of admiration, but only of punishment. For the word or the logic of the cross is indeed foolishness to those who are perishing, but to those who are saved—that is, to us—it is the power of God. Truly, what this "word of the cross" itself is, and what that logic of the most learned man is, this is the great, or rather the greatest and most secret mystery of the wonderful word. Of which...

<summary>This page argues that the power of the Name of Jesus is inextricably linked to the symbol of the Cross, using biblical typology (Moses, Ezekiel) and linguistic analysis of Hebrew to frame the Cross as a "secret doctrine" or "wondrous wood." It warns that attempting to use the Name without the Cross leads to failure or spiritual danger, citing the Sons of Sceva.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the dialogue of "The Wonder-Working Word" (De Verbo Mirifico), featuring the final instructions of the character Capnion (the pseudonym for Johannes Reuchlin) to his companions Sidonius and Baruchia, followed by a list of printer's corrections.</meta>

# ON THE WONDER-WORKING WORD

Dionysius the Areopagite <note>A 5th-century Christian Neoplatonist philosopher, often cited by Reuchlin to justify mystical interpretations.</note> mentions this briefly in his *Ecclesiastical Hierarchy*. Since it is proper not to scatter these secret veils and most hidden symbols into the open air, but rather to whisper them into the ear, I wish for you to come closer, Sidonius, so that I may breathe this inspiration into you. Do you hold it? And he replied, "I hold it." Then **CAPNION** said: Keep silent, hide it, conceal it, cover it, be still, mutter it not. And you, **BARUCHIA**, lend your ear likewise nonetheless. Have you received it correctly? And he said, "I have indeed, quite well." And to you also, **CAPNION** says, I forbid you to betray this to the common people. For whatever you seek in this manner shall be done for you. Now, therefore, I shall depart. Fare you well in good health, and revere the wonder-working word <note>original: "verbum mirificum"; referring to the five-lettered name of Jesus (Pentagrammaton) which Reuchlin argued was the miraculous completion of the Hebrew Tetragrammaton.</note> most divinely.
